# Canonical-index and membership-set memory census — 2026-11-11

All byte figures below are **bytes**, not MB. Census statistics come from real data; counterfactual layouts are estimates, not implemented formats or measured peak GPU memory. Full source plans, physical layout details, per-column statistics, domain maps and provenance are embedded in the JSON.

## Answer and coverage

TPC-H: 52 query/scale instances, 290 structures, complete cardinality and comparison-footprint accounting. 48 canonical retained capacities remain unobserved; their live keys/counts are known, not zero.
DOOP xalan: 103 relations, 103 canonical full sets, 120 additional index orders, and 2 negative keysets. Exact statistics come from real EDB facts, full GPU witnesses and CPU evaluation of remaining acyclic rules. All final relation row counts and layout live-byte aggregates match the revision-matched GPU trace; non-exported IDB column statistics are CPU-derived, not separately downloaded from the GPU.

A bitmap-only policy is not a universal replacement. Scalar bitmaps may replace membership keys or a directory **with rank, offsets and payload retained**. Multi-column identity generally needs an enormous product bitmap; binary adaptive containers are a different representation. Cost-aware selection is required.

## Workload totals

TPC-H totals sum independent query inventories, not simultaneous GPU residency. The current column is a published/accounted footprint: known allocated buffers plus exact canonical live keys/counts where capacity is unknown. Candidate totals use the same structures and keep current when no modeled alternative helps. Shared maps are paid once per query, never once per structure or silently shared across queries.

| Workload | Structures | Current accounted/owner bytes | Best modeled bytes incl. selected maps | Bitmap-only cost-aware bytes incl. maps | Forced raw-span scalar bitmaps |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
|TPC-H SF1|145|542,887,598|283,528,970|298,366,721|301,114,997|
|TPC-H SF10|145|6,195,486,561|3,155,515,730|3,310,322,631|3,332,825,450|
|DOOP xalan (logical owners)|225|432,996,160|162,342,580|428,405,104|432,427,184|

The TPC-H inventory includes the recorded host-side H02 `SortedRun`: 2,348,444 bytes at SF1 and 23,661,964 at SF10. These are not GPU-resident allocations. All other provider placements and exact stored widths are identified in the structure tables.

**DOOP physical live ownership bounds:** 271,215,680–370,511,608 bytes, versus the larger owner sum above. Fresh same-order batches alias; independently merged batches need not. Bounds include both negative keysets. They exclude retained old snapshots, allocator caches, build scratch and query peak. The candidate owner sum is a conservative no-alias upper accounting scenario, not measured residency.

The bitmap-only cost-aware column permits any profitable modeled bitmap, including small Cartesian domains; it excludes FOR/u32/adaptive alternatives. Forced scalar bitmap totals replace every scalar-key structure with its raw-span bitmap even when this loses; other structures remain unchanged. The JSON also records the stricter `span <= 32*distinct` policy and conditional full-shared-domain scalar totals.

## Structure classes and bitmap applicability

| Workload | Single dense key | Single sparse key | Multi-column | Bag/occurrence or other | Scalar raw bitmap candidates | Profitable scalar raw bitmaps | span/d <=32 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
|TPC-H SF1|32|7|29|77|100|84|92|
|TPC-H SF10|32|7|29|77|100|89|92|
|DOOP xalan|7|24|194|0|31|12|7|

Classes describe raw observed spans/identity and storage obligations, not private dense interning. Payload-bearing structures retain payload even when their key domain is dense. Exact classification and set/bag flags are recorded per structure.

## DOOP domains, word types and adaptive binary sets

Physical word types across all layouts/keysets: `{'u64': 152, '[u64; 3]': 22, 'u128': 49, '[u64; 4]': 2}`. All use implicit unit offsets; emitted radix directories have zero entries. Per-run/field/count metadata are included, not an invented 4-byte offset per tuple.

The existing global IStr universe has 2,830,273 codes. The string dictionary is already host-resident and is not uploaded as a new GPU dictionary. There are 32 additional measured rule-variable equality components available as optional shared semantic domains.
All semantic maps would cost 16,824,864 bytes. The exact useful-map subset search selects ['doop.semantic.19.type'] for 46,628 bytes, yielding 162,342,580 bytes including maps, versus 162,432,936 without new semantic maps. These maps support binary-search translation, not an assumed free O(1) lookup.

Using only bitmaps plus current fallback instead selects ['doop.semantic.19.type', 'doop.semantic.22.method', 'doop.semantic.25.method_modifier.c0'], paying 454,380 bytes in maps; total 428,405,104. Thus broad bitmap replacement barely changes DOOP memory compared with the much larger FOR/adaptive benefit.

## Complete structure tables

Candidate columns choose the smallest no-remap option in that family where available; `+ map` means the displayed data bytes additionally require shared maps. **Best workload totals above charge maps exactly once.** A dash means not modeled/applicable, never zero. Raw and dense bitmap alternatives, all byte components and physical-versus-logical row distinctions remain in JSON.

## Provenance and uncertainty

TPC-H plan source: `/data/mini_linq/experiments/gpu/target/release/build/cq-gpu-tpch-6133e28ae20ab725/out/plans.json`; SHA-256 `4baf3cea9f29ffc3f13ea17ead981b37004702b897a1b4c6b7266af941a2b12d`. All 52 accepted advised query/scale plans and their Structures text are retained in JSON.
DOOP measured revision: `1c3931f120808ff5892f953b1fa424d6b93463eb`; binary SHA-256 `a32952fd5770116959885d997fdbd987a1bc6126184b4ce026731d4834f3a0aa`. All 103 relation counts match exact reconstructed tuples. Layout byte aggregates match revision-matched trace records.

The remaining uncertainty is explicitly allocation-related: 48 TPC-H canonical pre-dedup capacities, DOOP noncanonical capacity slack, old snapshots, allocator/cache residency and exact IDB alias sharing are not inferred from row counts. No full-heap or peak-memory claim is made. Candidate savings are layout-model differences, not a deployed memory reduction.

TPC-H and DOOP candidate headers/rank/checkpoint profiles differ and are specified in each source record. They are feasible accounting profiles, not claims that either workload already implements them.

Reproduction helpers: `tpch_index_census.py`, `doop_index_census.py`, and `index_census_report.py` in `experiments/storage-bench/gpu/`. DOOP build/run/source provenance and failed attempts are embedded in JSON; local measurement artifacts are source evidence, not engine edits.

```sh
flock -s /tmp/mini_linq-timing.lock env CUDA_HOME=/opt/nvidia/hpc_sdk/Linux_x86_64/25.9/cuda/13.0 BINDGEN_EXTRA_CLANG_ARGS=-I/opt/nvidia/hpc_sdk/Linux_x86_64/25.9/math_libs/13.0/targets/x86_64-linux/include cargo +1.99.1 oxide build --arch sm_89 --materialize-cubin -- --release --bin doop-gpu
```

## Interpretation and accounting rules

- **A set's size is not its domain size.** A bitmap over an existing shared domain with `D` positions costs `4 * ceil(D/32)` bytes. Ranking a privately interned set into `[0,n)` does not make its dictionary or membership translation free. Raw-span and shared dense-domain bitmaps must be distinguished.
- **Payload and multiplicity survive representation changes.** A membership bitmap alone cannot replace a bag or a payload-bearing index. A grouped directory can instead use membership bits, a rank prefix, compact group offsets when groups have multiple rows, and all original payload rows. For a unique key with one payload row, rank can directly identify the payload position.
- **Rank is not free.** The measured E-a representation stores one u32 prefix per bitmap word: bitmap plus rank costs `8 * ceil(D/32)` bytes, before payload/offsets. This is not the size of a more succinct rank implementation.
- **Composite identity is not a scalar key domain.** A full tuple bitmap needs the Cartesian product of its shared column domains, even when only a tiny subset of those tuples occurs. Binary adaptive structures avoid allocating the entire product by grouping on the first key and using per-group sorted containers or bitmap chunks. Their directory and chunk metadata still count.
- **Candidate profiles are explicit estimates.** The DOOP model uses u64 bitmap words and one u64 rank checkpoint per 512 bits (plus a terminal checkpoint), not E-a's more expensive per-word rank. Within-chunk popcounts supply the remaining rank. Its binary adaptive model uses shared-code 2^16 chunks, u16 arrays versus 8192-byte bitmaps, explicit group/chunk headers, and rank metadata. These formats are not implemented or timed by this census; per-record byte breakdowns are authoritative.
- **Current physical representations are not uniformly plain u64.** TPC-H's `RangeProvider` persists encoded offsets, physical payload columns and a `ColumnView` array; temporary sort keys and row permutations are released. Leading key columns can be implicit in the directory and absent from physical payload. DOOP's canonical packed word can be narrower than an array of u32 columns.
- **Provider role matters.** A Structures declaration `rows = @ range_provider` can lower to flat retained-prefix rows and expansion begin/offset arrays rather than the `RangeProvider` type used by a keyed build. The census follows generated physical storage, distinguishes retained prefixes from logically expanded rows, and does not infer bytes from the provider token alone.
- **Allocation and useful encoded extent differ.** `EncodedColumn::from_device` allocates from global bounds and capacity even when per-vector FOR headers describe smaller live extents. `RangeProvider::bytes()` includes allocated encoded column buffers and payload views, not allocator overhead or shared count-owner allocations. Do not compare only a hypothetical compact extent against an allocated current representation without naming that distinction.
- **Workload totals are inventory sums.** Per-query TPC-H structures are independent instances; summing the suite does not imply all queries' indexes coexist. Aliased/view-only structures must not be counted as additional allocations. Transient sorting/build scratch, base columns, answer publication and host allocator overhead are separate from persistent index/set storage.
- **Eligibility is not a recommendation.** A forced bitmap can grow memory for small sets over large domains. A cost-aware policy retains the existing or another compressed representation when the bitmap is larger. Unknown cardinalities or unknown payload sizes must remain unknown rather than silently contributing zero to totals.

### Break-even checks (ignoring small headers and final-word rounding)

These algebraic thresholds are model results, not measured workload counts:

| Replacement | Bitmap-side bytes | Bitmap is smaller when |
|---|---:|---|
| Membership-only sorted u32 keys | `D/8` versus `4n` | `D/n < 32` |
| Membership-only sorted u64 keys | `D/8` versus `8n` | `D/n < 64` |
| u32 keys requiring a rank position | `D/4` versus `4n` | `D/n < 16` |
| u64 keys requiring a rank position | `D/4` versus `8n` | `D/n < 32` |
| One 2^16 adaptive chunk, u16 array versus bitmap | `8192` versus `2c` | `c > 4096` |
| Plain dense u32 bag directory, payload unchanged | `D/4 + 4g` versus `4D` | `g/D < 15/16` |

Here `D` is the shared domain size, `n` is set cardinality, `c` is a chunk's cardinality, and `g` is the number of occupied group keys. The last row includes bitmap, per-word rank and compact u32 group offsets; it shows why an almost-fully-occupied **directory** can grow when replaced by bitmap+rank+offsets even though a standalone key array shrinks. Existing FOR-encoded directories require the actual codec accounting, not this plain-directory threshold.

## Dense interning is a separate memory decision

An independent scan of the real primary-key files verified sortedness, row counts, distinct counts and min/max. The order-key universe is sparse by a factor of four; part and customer primary-key universes are contiguous at both scale factors.

| Universe | Distinct | Raw min..max | Raw-domain bitmap bytes | Dense-domain bitmap bytes | New sorted-u32 dictionary bytes, **if that implementation is chosen** |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
| SF1 orders.o_orderkey | 1,500,000 | 1..6,000,000 | 750,000 | 187,500 | 6,000,000 |
| SF10 orders.o_orderkey | 15,000,000 | 1..60,000,000 | 7,500,000 | 1,875,000 | 60,000,000 |
| SF1 part.p_partkey | 200,000 | 1..200,000 | 25,000 | 25,000 | unnecessary: subtract the minimum |
| SF10 part.p_partkey | 2,000,000 | 1..2,000,000 | 250,000 | 250,000 | unnecessary: subtract the minimum |
| SF1 customer.c_custkey | 150,000 | 1..150,000 | 18,752 | 18,752 | unnecessary: subtract the minimum |
| SF10 customer.c_custkey | 1,500,000 | 1..1,500,000 | 187,500 | 187,500 | unnecessary: subtract the minimum |

Thus an isolated SF10 order-domain bitset is **7.5 MB** without new interning, versus **61.875 MB** if dense interning is implemented with a newly allocated 60 MB sorted-u32 dictionary plus the 1.875 MB bitset. This is an implementation-cost example, not an information-theoretic lower bound: an existing shared map, compressed dictionary or certified arithmetic mapping has a different cost. A shared map is charged once to its owning catalog, not once per index. Conditional dense-domain bitmap figures must not silently assume that such a map already exists.

Source: manifest-specified `orders.o_orderkey.bin`, `part.p_partkey.bin`, and `customer.c_custkey.bin` under `/data/tpch-gpu/sf1` and `/data/tpch-gpu/sf10`; exact measured universe statistics are preserved in the JSON.

## Source anchors for the accounting model

- `experiments/gpu/sequence-runtime/src/lib.rs:872-923`: duplicate-preserving `RangeProvider`, its fields and byte metric.
- `experiments/gpu/sequence-runtime/src/lib.rs:1394-1424`: dense lower-bound directory construction, FOR offset publication, physical payload packing and release of temporary keys/permutations.
- `experiments/gpu/device-columns/src/device.rs:34-59`: 32-byte `VectorHeader`; 40-byte `ColumnView` on the measured 64-bit host/device ABI.
- `experiments/gpu/device-columns/src/lib.rs:402-425,682-685`: device encoding allocation from bounds/capacity and the encoded-column byte metric.
- `experiments/storage-bench/gpu/compressed_common.cuh`: E-a candidate key-block accounting: 16-byte frame plus u32 fence per 1024 keys; bitmap with one u32 rank prefix per word.

The E-a/E-b timing results are in `gpu-2026-11-11-compressed-ops.{md,json}`. They do not establish this census's memory totals and are not substituted for real workload cardinalities.
